## Disentangled generative modeling of single -cell perturbation responses 1

## across dynamic cellular states 2

Authors:  Yue Wang 1,†, Yueshan Zhao 1, Sihan Li 1, Da Yang 1,2,3

ABSTRACT 12 

Single -cell perturbation studies are increasingly conducted across complex, multi -condition 13 

biological systems, offering a powerful lens into how interventions reshape cell states and mo-14 

lecular programs. However, standard computational models compress single -cell profiles into 15 

unified representations where treatment effects are obscured by cell division, lineage progres-16 

sion, donor variation, and cellular stress.  Here, w e present Disentangled Variational Inference 17 

for Perturbation Response (PertVI), a deep generative model that separates baseline mitotic 18 

phase, cellular state, and batch effects from  treatment -induced shifts while isolating a berrant 19 

off -manifold responses  in orthogonal anomaly space. This structured design tackles two inter-20 

twined tasks:  (1)  quantifying  treatment -induced  changes in cell -state composition , and  (2)  in-21 

ferring state -gated  transcriptional  changes in  response  to treatment . Across simulated bench-22 

marks,  PertVI accurately disentangles coupled biological  axes  and recovers weak treatment 23 

signatures . In  conditional chemical and genetic  screens of primary human  CD8+  and  CD4+  T24 

cells, PertVI separates memory -precursor adjuvants from  toxic  phenocopies, resolves co -clus-25 

tered regulators into directional kinetic trajectories  and biphasic rebounders  obscured by stand-26 

ard workflows , and  reveals that  treatment -induced  phenotypic  shifting is decoupled from broad 27 

transcriptional perturbation responses and  instead  governed  by sta te-specific impulses . Alto-28 

gether,  PertVI brings  unique  mechanistic granularity to  single -cell  perturbation response mod-29 

eling , serving as  a proof  of  principle for biology -informed, structure -guided variance disentan-30 

glement in dynamic cellular systems. 31

INTRODUCTION 34 

Over the past decade, single -cell perturbation screens have rapidly expanded from uniform cell 35 

lines 1-8 into dynamic, multi -condition biological systems 9-15 , providing unprecedented oppor-36 

tunities to decode how gene regulatory networks, transcriptional regulons, and molecular in-37 

teractomes rewire across physiological contexts. Realizing the full potential of these multi -38 

layered perturbation screens, however, requires rethinking how to leverage the expressive ca-39 

pacity of deep architectures to uncover the complex biological relationships these datasets are 40 

built to reveal. Early analytical frameworks relied on a static cellular baseline where treatment 41 

effects dominate, whereas in dynamic, multi -condition systems, localized perturbation shifts 15 -

42  17  are overshadowed by macroscopic state transitions, periodic cell -cycle oscillations 18 -23 , and 43 

donor or batch variation 17,24 -28 . Moreover, these sources of variation are usually biologically 44 

intertwined: proliferation covaries with lineage progression and activation, while perturbations 45 

simultaneously alter division rates, shift cell -state composition, modulate state -dependent gene 46 

programs, and trigger cellular stress 2,6,7,13,15 . This pervasive variance entanglement poses fun-47 

damental challenges for perturbation analysis in modern multi -condition screens. 48 

Interrogating dynamic, multi -condition perturbation screens centers on two fundamental ana-49 

lytical tasks. The first task is to determine whether an intervention pushes, arrests, or redirects 50 

cells along permissible biological trajectories, such as accelerating differentiation, halting mi-51 

totic progression,  or  shifting functional state occupancy across developing tissues and primary 52 

cohorts 9,10,12 -14 . The second task is to resolve how a cell ’s underlying baseline state  or  environ-53 

mental context govern its transcriptional response to a perturbation, uncovering regulatory cir-54 

cuits that act selectively across developmental stages, co -cultures,  or  stimulation re-55 

gimes 6,11,15,29 . Accomplishing both tasks amid confounding proliferation and non -physiological 56 

toxicity exposes three  core  challenges that monolithic latent embeddings 30 -34  and purely unsu-57 

pervised factorization models 19,35 -37  struggle to resolve. 58 

First, isolating perturbation effects in dynamic systems requires an unconfounded baseline rep-59 

resentation that separates periodic cell division from progressive differentiation or activation 60 

states. Standard preprocessing pipelines score canonical cell -cycle marker sets 18,38  and regress 61 

them out of expression matrices using linear or empirical Bayes models 24,39,40 . Whenever pro-62 

liferation covaries with state transitions, orthogonal linear projection subtracts correlated de-63 

velopmental signals from pleiotropic metabolic and signaling genes, distorting the underlying 64 

state trajectory 21,22,27 . Conversely, specialized circular projection algorithms reconstruct con-65 

tinuous mitotic phase angles on two -dimensional ring manifolds 20,22 , but operate as standalone 66 

projections that neither infer a deconfounded cellular state manifold nor model perturbation 67 

effect.  Meanwhile, deep generative frameworks based on monolithic variational autoencod-68 

ers 30,41 , unsupervised factorized bottlenecks 37 , and contrastive or multi -resolution architec-69 

tures 32,41,42  encode the unperturbed baseline as a single undivided space, allowing mitotic os-70 

cillations to fragment developmental trajectories. 71 

Second, relative to a cell ’s baseline state, perturbations act through two distinct biological 72 

modes that current frameworks fail to disentangle. A perturbation can act as a state -driving 73 

intervention that accelerates, arrests, or redirects cellular occupancy along a baseline trajectory, 74 

or as a state -driven intervention whose transcriptional effect is conditioned on a cell ’s local 75 

baseline state. Population -level differential expression (DE) models 43 -45  and replicate -level 76 

pseudobulk workflows 46,47  average across heterogeneous cells, conflating compositional shifts 77 

in state occupancy with  state -dependent  transcriptional r esponse . Conversely, when tracking 78 

compositional changes, trajectory estimators 48 -52  and  graph -based  differential abundance meth-79 

ods 53 -55  construct cell -similarity graphs on  the total  embeddings,  naturally  conflating genuine 80 

abundance shifts with perturbation -induced topological distortions.  Deep generative 81 

> preprint (which was not certified by peer review) is the author/funder. All rights reserved. No reuse allowed without permission. The copyright holder for this this version posted November 10, 2026. ;https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.11.07.765083 doi: bioRxiv preprint 3

perturbation models 31,33,34,56,57 , on the other hand,  commonly  lack a perturbation -agnostic base-82 

line re ference,  in evitabl y entanglin g intervention -driven abundance shifts with state -driven 83 

transcriptional responses. 84 

Third, genetic or chemical perturbations that disrupt essential survival, metabolic, or homeo-85 

static pathways frequently trigger acute cellular stress and cytotoxicity that drive transcrip-86 

tomes off the physiological baseline manifold. Rather than modulating homeostatic lineage or 87 

activation programs, such interventions activate conserved endoplasmic reticulum stress and 88 

the unfolded protein response 2, proteotoxic or oxidative stress pathways 6,58 , or apoptotic and 89 

viability -linked cytotoxic programs 12,58 . Separating these non -physiological stress responses 90 

from homeostatic state modulation is critical for distinguishing selective biological regulators 91 

and therapeutic adjuvants from toxic interventions that merely arrest growth or mimic quies-92 

cence. However, existing compositional and additive latent -shift models 33 , contrastive and 93 

multi -resolution salient -space architectures 32,41,42 , and optimal -transport frameworks 29,59  oper-94 

ate under a closed -world manifold assumption that enforces no geometric boundary between 95 

on -manifold physiological transitions and off -manifold  toxicity . Absorbing both homeostatic 96 

transitions and acute cellular  stress  into unconstrained perturbation embeddings, current meth-97 

ods  can hardly distinguish whether an intervention pushes the cells along permitted, on -mani-98 

fold trajectory or induce aberrant, off -manifold toxicity .99 

To address these challenges as a proof of principle, we introduce Disentangled Variational 100 

Inference for Perturbation Response (PertVI), a structurally disentangled counterfactual gen-101 

erative framework that resolves variance entanglement by replacing monolithic latent compres-102 

sion with biology -informed, separated manifolds. PertVI uses three perturbation -blind encod-103 

ers to factorize each cell ’s transcriptome into a low -dimensional circular cell -cycle anchor 60 , a 104 

macroscopic cellular state anchor, and an origin -anchored out -of -distribution anomaly sub-105 

space, while an isolated linear decoder head absorbs donor and batch variation. Structural sep-106 

aration is enforced by decoding the cell -cycle subspace through a hard gene -marker mask 18 ,107 

penalizing cell -cycle weights in the main nonlinear decoder, and applying minibatch cross -108 

covariance repulsion across subspaces. Perturbation identity enters exclusively after inference 109 

through a Split Feature -wise Linear Modulation module 61  that applies perturbation -specific af-110 

fine transformations to the cell -cycle and state anchors, whereas non -linear off -manifold  pa-111 

thology  bypass es  this transition and route s into the anomaly subspace.  This architecture sup-112 

ports both unbiased differential abundance analysis on the perturbation -blind baseline mani-113 

folds and single -cell counterfactual differential expression across modulated states. Together, 114 

these components provide a unique multi -layered representation for single -cell perturbation 115 

data while delivering state -of -the -art performance in sample integration specifically designed 116 

for perturbation experiments. On the software side, PertVI leverages the optimization proce-117 

dures and probabilistic modeling ecosystem of scvi -tools 62 , allowing it to scale to large -scale 118 

perturbation screens. 119 

In this work, we first validate PertVI on simulated multi -covariate benchmarks with known 120 

ground truth, demonstrating that it simultaneously harmonizes batches, reconstructs circular 121 

cell -cycle topology and continuous differentiation trajectories, and recovers weak and target -122 

overlapping perturbation effects where existing methods fail. We then  apply  PertVI  to two 123 

primary human T -cell screens in which all three entanglement regimes converge. In a chemical 124 

screen of differentiating  CD8+  T cells 63 , PertVI distinguishes T -cell -sparing oncology drugs 125 

from conserved cell -cycle blockers, uncovers stage -restricted differentiation catalysts whose 126 

fate shifts are decoupled from global transcriptional magnitude, and separates genuine 127 

memory -precursor adjuvants from toxic phenocopies. In a multi -condition CRISPR interfer-128 

ence screen of  CD4+  T cells across resting and stimulated states 15 , PertVI resolves unsigned 129 

perturbation clusters into signed kinetic trajectories, uncovering tonic checkpoint brakes and 130 

> preprint (which was not certified by peer review) is the author/funder. All rights reserved. No reuse allowed without permission. The copyright holder for this this version posted November 10, 2026. ;https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.11.07.765083 doi: bioRxiv preprint 4

biphasic homeostatic regulators that delay early activation but rebound into late effector hyper -131 

activation, while preventing proliferative arrest from confounding T -cell activation phenotypes. 132

RESULTS 134 

Disentangled variational inference for perturbation response 135 

Conventional deep generative integration  approache s and  dimensional ity  reduction  techniques 136 

usually  compress  whole -transcriptom ic  variance into  a single, monolithic latent space. In 137 

highly proliferative systems  like cancer cells and immune cells , however,  this monolithic com-138 

pression encounters a fundamental analytical bottleneck: high -amplitude transcriptional oscil-139 

lations driven by the cell cycle routinely dominate the latent embedding, obscur ing other  subtle 140 

intrinsic cellular processes  like metabolism and  differentiation. Furthermore, in perturbation 141 

genomics, an entangled baseline mathematically conflates expression changes governed by in-142 

trinsic cellular  state shift  with state -driving perturbation momentum, making it difficult to de-143 

termine whether a candidate perturbagen actively accelerates a permitted developmental tran-144 

sition or induces an aberrant stress response  (Fig. 1 a).145 

PertVI addresses this dual challenge by enforcing two levels of mathematical disentanglement 146 

(Fig. 1 b and c ). First, at the covariate level, PertVI  learn s a  universal,  perturbation -agnostic 147 

baseline ( 𝑢 𝑏𝑎𝑠𝑒 ) that represents where a cell resides on the natural biological landscape , serving 148 

as baseline anchors.  Within this baseline, a semantic filtration system physically quarantines 149 

high -amplitude periodic cell -cycle variance ( 𝑢 𝑐𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒 ) away from the macroscopic cellular state 150 

backbone ( 𝑢 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒 ). Second, at the causal perturbation level,  PertVI  utilizes  Feature -wise Linear 151 

Modulation (FiLM) 61  to  restrict  “on -manifold ” perturbation  effect  to an affine transcriptomic 152 

momentum ( 𝑧 𝑐 𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒 , 𝑧 𝑠 𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒  ) conditioned  on  the  baseline  anchor  (𝑢 𝑐 𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒 , 𝑢 𝑠 𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒  ) (see  Methods ). 153 

Because  FiLM  is  mathematically  constrained to linear scaling and shifting along permitted 154 

biological axes, it cannot accommodate topological ruptures; consequently, PertVI routes any 155 

residual, non -linear perturbation variance , such as synthetic stress  and  toxicity , into a dedicated 156 

out -of -distribution novelty space ( 𝑣 𝑜𝑜𝑑 ) that the natural baseline anchor cannot explain. 157 

By decoupling the perturbation -agnostic baseline manifold  𝑢 𝑏𝑎𝑠𝑒  from the perturbation -aware 158 

affine shift  𝑧 𝑏𝑎𝑠𝑒 , a trained PertVI model enables two complementary analytical regimes that 159 

overcome the  current  limitations of conventional single -cell perturbation pipelines  (Fig. 1 d160 

and e ).161 

In the first regime  (Fig. 1d ), the baseline manifolds ( 𝑢 𝑐𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒  and  𝑢 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒  ) serve as an unbiased 162 

coordinate canvas for investigating cell -cycle progression,  constructing  developmental trajec-163 

tories, and  performing  Differential Abundance (DA ) analysis . Conventional differential abun-164 

dance methods rely on clustering or integrating treated and control cells into a shared latent 165 

space , which is  prone to algorithmic over -correction that erases genuine treatment -induced bi-166 

ological shifts, or under -correction that leaves residual batch artifacts. Because PertVI learns a 167 

universal baseline manifold using probabilistic encoders that operate strictly blind to perturba-168 

tion labels during inference, mapping treated and control populations onto  𝑢 𝑐𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒  and  𝑢 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒 169 

allows for the rigorous quantification of shifts in phenotypic occupancy , such as drug -induced 170 

lineage selection  or  cell -cycle arrest, without confounding from supervised label leakage or 171 

integration artifacts. 172 

In the second regime  (Fig. 1e ), the perturbation -aware latent space s 𝑧 𝑐 𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒  and  𝑧 𝑠 𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒  enable 173 

two kinds of  counterfactual Differential Expression (cDE)  analyses : state -specific responses 174 

and state -shifting momentum. In standard observational  DE  analyses, comparing treated and 175 

control populations inevitably conflates p erturbation -induc ed expression changes  with baseline 176 

> preprint (which was not certified by peer review) is the author/funder. All rights reserved. No reuse allowed without permission. The copyright holder for this this version posted November 10, 2026. ;https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.11.07.765083 doi: bioRxiv preprint 5

covariate imbalances and  perturbation -induced  population  shifts. PertVI resolves this con-177 

founding by holding an identical basal coordinate anchor ( 𝑢 𝑏𝑎𝑠𝑒 ) constant while substituting 178 

alternative treatment conditions into the  FiLM  engine . By generating  counterfactual trajecto-179 

ries , PertVI allows the investigation on  whether a perturbagen induces static, state -specific 180 

transcriptional signatures within an isolated sub -population or exerts active, state -shifting  per-181 

turbation  momentum that propels cells along permitted Waddington trajectories. 182 

A comprehensive  description of  mathematical formulation of the PertVI architecture, objective 183 

function governance, and  the  post -training counterfactual analytical  framework  is provided in 184 

the  Methods .185 

Disentangling perturbation response from cellular baseline on a synthetic dataset 186 

To validate the  structural  disentanglement capabilities of PertVI, we benchmarked the archi-187 

tecture against conventional  analytical  too ls  (Linear Discriminant Analysis, LDA;  COMBAT 64 )188 

and  the  deep generative models  for data integration and variance disentanglement 189 

(LinearSCVI 35 , MrVI 41 , DrVI 37 , contrastiveVI 32 , and MoCAVI 42 ) using a synthetic simulation 190 

with ground -truth sources of variation ( Fig. 2a , b; Extended Data Fig. 1 ; See  Methods ). The 191 

synthetic  dataset comprised 10,000 cells and 100 genes encoding four concurrent sources of 192 

transcriptomic variance in a hierarchical structure, quantified as mean per -gene variance frac-193 

tions on log -normalized counts: cell differentiation state (19.6%), cell -cycle progression 194 

(10.9%), sequencing batch (4.6%), and transcriptional perturbation (3.6%). Cell -cycle expres-195 

sion was modeled as a continuous half -sinusoidal oscillation across three phases  (G1 -S-G2M) ,196 

with phase -specific gene expression profiles designed to mimic the ring topology of the cell 197 

cycle  (Extended Data Fig. 1a -b). Differentiation state was encoded as a continuous trajectory 198 

through three ordered states  (State1 -State2 -State3)  using tent -function basis functions, with a 199 

peak log -foldchange  (LFC)  of 5.0 relative to a cell -cycle peak of 3.5  (Extended Data Fig. 1c 200 

and d ). Four perturbations were constructed with a defined similarity structure  (Fig. 2b ; Ex-201 

tended Data Fig. 1e and f ): Pert -A and Pert -B were strong perturbations each targeting 50 202 

genes, with 90% gene -level overlap between them; Pert -C was a weak perturbation targeting 203 

18 genes drawn exclusively from loci not targeted by Pert -A or Pert -B; Pert -D introduced neg-204 

ligible genome -wide noise as a near -null control. Counts were sampled from a Zero -Inflated 205 

Negative Binomial model, yielding a dataset with 41.2% sparsity and a mean of 6,263 UMIs 206 

per cell  (Extended Data Fig. 1g ). In addition, a  bidirectional cross -covariate coupling term 207 

between state and cell -cycle marker genes was included to reflect the partial non -orthogonality 208 

inherent to biological data  (Extended Data Fig. 1h and i ). In this design, perturbation is the 209 

weakest and most confounded variance component, representing a benchmark condition in 210 

which monolithic latent representations are expected to fail . To benchmark, a ll model -based 211 

approaches were trained on identical train/test splits with matched batch sizes and learning 212 

rates; methods requiring hyperparameter optimization underwent grid search prior to evalua-213 

tion.  A detailed description of the model training, the parameter settings, and the performance 214 

is provided in the  Methods  and supplementary materials ( Extended Data Fig. 2a ).215 

We  first  assessed whether each method could recover the baseline biological manifold from 216 

the four concurrent variance sources ( Fig. 2 c).  Disentanglement performance was quantified 217 

using adversarial F1 (aF1) and LISI scores computed independently for each of the four 218 

ground -truth variance sources  (Fig. 2d; Extended Data Fig. 2b ; see  Methods ). Batch  integra-219 

tion  was assessed first as a prerequisite filter:  all methods except LinearSCVI achieved ade-220 

quate batch mixing (aF1 < 0.8 0; LISI > 0.2 0), compared to the uncorrected embedding (aF1 = 221 

0.99; LISI = 0.01); LinearSCVI was therefore excluded from further disentanglement evalua-222 

tion. Among the remaining methods, only PertVI resolved the cell -cycle and cell -state mani-223 

folds into separate, interpretable subspaces  (Fig. 2e; Extended Fig. 2c ). The  perturbation -224

agnostic  cell -cycle encoder ( 𝑢 𝑐𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒 ), constrained to a low -dimensional topology with a relaxed 225 

prior, reconstructed the continuous ring structure of the cell cycle, with G1, S, and G2M pop-226 

ulations forming smooth, overlapping arcs fully aligned with the ground -truth cell -cycle 227 

pseudotime gradient ( Fig. 2 c, “Cell Cycle ” and  “Cell Cycle  𝜑 ”, PertVI  𝑢 𝑐𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒 ; aF1 =  0.90,  LISI 228 

= 0.04 for the cell -cycle variable in  𝑢 𝑐𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒 ). The  perturbation -agnostic  macroscopic state en-229 

coder ( 𝑢 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒 ), operating  without  any prior knowledge of differentiation state, autonomously 230 

resolved the three ground -truth states as an ordered trajectory  (aF1 =  0.98,  LISI =  0.00  for the 231 

cell -state  variable in  𝑢 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒  ), with the cell -cycle pseudotime gradient absent from the state axis 232 

(Fig. 2 c, “Cell State ” and  “Cell State  𝜏 ”, PertVI  𝑢 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒 ; aF1 =  0.59,  LISI =  0.36  for the cell -233 

cycle variable in  𝑢 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒  ). No competing method achieved this dual resolution. 234 

COMBAT, applied with perturbation as covariate, left cell -cycle and cell -state signals entan-235 

gled within a single corrected space  (Fig. 2c and d , “Cell Cycle ” and  “Cell State ”, COMBAT) .236 

LDA  (Linear Discriminant Analysis)  separated perturbation clusters but conflated cell -cycle 237 

and cell -state variance within the same projection, with perturbation effects dominating the 238 

embedding  (Fig. 2c and d , “Cell Cycle ” and  “Cell State ”, LDA) . MrVI ’s sample -unaware  𝑢 239 

space removed perturbation -driven variance from the biological background but could not par-240 

tition cell -cycle from cell -state variance within a single continuous representation; its sample -241 

aware  𝑧  space further failed to isolate perturbation from the cellular background  (Fig. 2c and 242 

d, “Cell Cycle ” and  “Cell State ”, MrVI  𝑢  and  𝑧 ). Both contrastive learning approaches , Con-243 

trastiveVI and MoCAVI , partitioned perturbation -induced variance into a salient space and 244 

biology into a background space  (Fig. 2c and d , ContrastiveVI and MoCAVI) ; however, the 245 

background space itself remained a joint, undifferentiated mixture of cell -cycle and cell -state 246 

variance with no further factorization.  The unsupervised approach  DrVI , on the other hand, 247 

distributed cell -state variance  across all factored subspaces, such that no individual split repre-248 

sented a unique variance source  (Fig. 2c and d , DrVI  with  n_split=3 and n_split=5;  Extended 249 

Data Fig. 2 d and 2 e). Importantly , visualizing PertVI ’s full concatenated representation ( 𝑧 𝑓𝑖𝑛𝑎𝑙 )250 

produced the same entangled topology observed in all competing methods ( Fig. 2 c, PertVI 251 

𝑧 𝑓𝑖𝑛𝑎𝑙 , compared to the salient spaces of ContrastiveVI and MoCAVI and the total  latent  space 252 

of DrVI ), confirming that signal conflation is an intrinsic consequence of joint compression 253 

and that upstream semantic filtration is necessary to recover interpretable biological subspaces. 254 

We next evaluated perturbation recovery across two complementary levels: the separation of 255 

perturbation conditions in latent embedding space, and the accuracy of gene -level perturbation 256 

effect estimation against the simulation ground truth  (see  Methods ). At the embedding level, 257 

five methods produced representations in which the four perturbation conditions separated 258 

from the non -targeting control: LDA, ContrastiveVI (salient space), MoCAVI (salient space), 259 

DrVI (total  𝑧 ), and PertVI ( perturbation -aware  𝑧 𝑐𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒 , 𝑧 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒  , 𝑧 𝑓𝑖𝑛𝑎𝑙 ) (Fig. 2 c and  d). Among 260 

these, only PertVI ’s perturbation -aware spaces achieved aF1 scores above 0.90 and LISI scores 261 

near zero for the perturbation variable ( 𝑧 𝑐𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒 : aF1 = 0.96, LISI = 0.04;  𝑧 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒  : aF1 = 1.00, LISI 262 

= 0.00;  𝑧 𝑓𝑖𝑛𝑎𝑙 : aF1 = 0.95, LISI = 0.09), indicating a substantially higher signal -to -noise sepa-263 

ration than any competing representation  (Fig. 2d; Extended Data Fig. 2b) . At the gene -level, 264 

we compared the inferred perturbation effects from methods with a generative or analytical 265 

decoder capable of producing gene -level output: LinearSCVI, ContrastiveVI, MoCAVI, and 266 

PertVI  (see  Methods ). Although LinearSCVI failed batch correction and was excluded from 267 

the embedding -level comparison, it provides gene -level perturbation effect estimates and was 268 

therefore included here for completeness.  Overall, e ach method ’s inferred gene -level effects 269 

were compared to the known LFCs for each of the four perturbations using Pearson correlation, 270 

alongside a standard differential expression  (DE)  baseline  (Fig. 2f -i). Among them, the s tand-271 

ard  DE  analysis failed to recover ground -truth LFC for all four perturbations  (Fig. 2f ).272 

> preprint (which was not certified by peer review) is the author/funder. All rights reserved. No reuse allowed without permission. The copyright holder for this this version posted November 10, 2026. ;https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.11.07.765083 doi: bioRxiv preprint 7

LinearSCVI recovered weak signal for the two strong perturbations (r = 0.31, Pert -A; r = 0.28, 273 

Pert -B) but showed negligible correlation for the weak and near -null perturbations (r = 0.11, 274 

Pert -C; r = 0.21, Pert -D)  (Extended Data Fig.2f ). ContrastiveVI , on the other hand,  produced 275 

more uniform recovery across perturbation strengths (r = 0.65, 0.66, 0.64, 0.22 for Pert -A,  -B, 276 

-C,  -D)  (Fig.2g ), while MoCAVI achieved high correlations for the two strong perturbations 277 

but failed on the weak and near -null ones (r = 0.87, 0.86, 0.21, 0.14  for Pert -A,  -B,  -C,  -D) 278 

(Fig.2h ). In contrast,  PertVI ’s perturbation  momentum achieved the highest correlation across 279 

all four perturbations (r = 0.90, 0.86, 0.82, 0.45 for Pert -A,  -B,  -C,  -D) and was the only method 280 

to recover meaningful signal for Pert -C, the weak perturbation targeting only 18 genes  (Fig.2i ).281 

This advantage likely stems from PertVI ’s Split -FiLM mechanism on the disentangled biolog-282 

ical axes : because a subset of  perturbation  target genes  is designed to  overlap with cell -cycle 283 

and cell -state marker genes  (Fig. 2b ) ( a common  scenari o in real perturbation studies, where 284 

drug targets frequently participate in cell -cycle regulation or differentiation programs ), model-285 

ing perturbation effects independently along each biological axis  conferred sensitivity to  weak 286 

signals that are otherwise masked by the dominant cellular variance.  Of note, a cross all meth-287 

ods that recovered perturbation signal, the within -benchmark similarity structure of the four 288 

perturbations (Pert -A ≈ Pert -B, Pert -C orthogonal, Pert -D near -null) was recapitulated in  all 289 

the respective  inferred gene -level perturbation effects  (Extended Data Fig. 2 g).  Taken to-290 

gether,  both  MoCAVI and ContrastiveVI represent capable approaches for gene -level pertur-291 

bation effect inference under strong perturbation regimes; PertVI extends this capacity to weak 292 

perturbations  and the  scenario  where target gene overlaps with endogenous biological pro-293 

grams require axis -separated modeling to achieve reliable signal recovery. 294 

Aggregating across all benchmarked criteria , i.e.,  batch correction, cell -cycle topology recon-295 

struction, differentiation state recovery, and perturbation signal recovery , PertVI was the only 296 

method to achieve adequate performance on all four axes simultaneously. Each competing 297 

method was limited by architectural constraints that produced a specific failure mode: 298 

LinearSCVI failed batch correction; COMBAT and LDA could not factorize cell -cycle from 299 

cell -state variance; MrVI, ContrastiveVI, and MoCAVI lacked dedicated encoders for the two 300 

baseline biological processes; DrVI distributed dominant variance sources across all split 301 

spaces without specificity; and no method other than PertVI recovered the weak perturbation 302 

signal against the confounded cellular background. These failure modes collectively reflect a 303 

fundamental limitation of monolithic and undifferentiated latent representations when applied 304 

to data in which multiple biological processes partially share gene targets and span a range of 305 

effect sizes. 306 

PertVI reveals heterogenous regulation by anti -cancer agents on CD8+ effector T cell 307 

differentiation 308 

Next,  we applied PertVI to a high -throughput  in vitro  pharmacological screen in primary hu-309 

man CD8+ T cells 63  (Fig. 3a ; see  Methods ). Th is  dataset profiles 62,207 peripheral blood 310 

mononuclear cell -derived CD8+ T cells from two healthy blood donors (Donor 1:  34,548  cells; 311 

Donor 2:  27,659  cells) across 62 small -molecule compounds (predominantly anti -cancer agents; 312 

55,735  treated cells) and dimethyl sulfoxide (DMSO) vehicle controls ( 6,472  cells). Naive 313 

CD8+ T cells were pre -incubated with each compound ( 1𝜇𝑀 ) across two biological replicates 314 

for 24 h, followed by 72 h of anti -CD3/CD28 co -stimulation and IL -2 treatment to induce 315 

clonal proliferation and effector differentiation across five developmental states: quiescent na-316 

ive T cells ( n = 16,060 ), early proliferating blasts ( n = 18,796 ), intermediate memory precursors 317 

(n = 5,076 ), cytolytic effectors ( n = 15,505 ), and terminal effectors ( n = 6,770 ). Primary single -318 

cell perturbation screens of this scale pose a fundamental analytical challenge: donor and rep-319 

licate variation, periodic cell -cycle progression, continuous lineage differentiation, and plei-320 

otropic drug responses are nonlinearly entangled within each cell ’s transcriptome. To resolve 321

these sources of variation within a unified causal framework ( Fig. 1 ; see  Methods ), we as-322 

signed the biological replicate identifier as the technical batch covariate ( 𝑏 𝑛 ) and compound 323 

identity as the target perturbation covariate ( 𝑝 𝑛 ). PertVI strips technical batch artifacts during 324 

initial encoding to infer two perturbation -agnostic single -cell baseline anchors: a 2D circular 325 

cell -cycle manifold ( 𝑢 

> cycle

) and a macroscopic developmental state manifold ( 𝑢 state ). On -man-326 

ifold pharmacological effects are modeled via a Split -FiLM transition that applies condition -327 

specific affine shifts independently to each baseline anchor ( 𝑧  

> cycle

and  𝑧 

> state

). Conversely, non -328 

homeostatic cellular stress and toxicity that depart from physiological trajectories are routed 329 

into an orthogonal out -of -distribution anomaly subspace ( 𝑣 

> ood

) before decoding through a bi-330 

furcated gene -expression architecture. Across 600 training and validation epochs, PertVI con-331 

verged smoothly without overfitting in evidence lower bound (ELBO), reconstruction loss, and 332 

total Kullback –Leibler (KL) divergence ( Extended Data Fig. 3a ). 333 

Benchmarking baseline disentanglement . Across all latent subspaces, PertVI harmonized do-334 

nor and replicate variation so that the perturbation -agnostic baseline anchors ( 𝑢 cycle , 𝑢 state ) and 335 

perturbation -aware spaces ( 𝑧 cycl 

> e

, 𝑧 state , 𝑧 final ) reflected biological and pharmacological varia-336 

tion rather than technical batch structure ( Extended Data Fig. 3b, c , “Donor ”; adversarial  F1 337 

(𝑎𝐹 1) =0.7 6 and Local Inverse Simpson ’s Index (LISI) =0.17  in  𝑧 final , with  aF1=0.5 3 to  0.72 338 

in  𝑢 cycle , 𝑧 cycle , and  𝑧 state ). In actively differentiating lymphocytes, periodic cell -cycle signals 339 

strongly covary with lineage progression. PertVI resolves this entanglement by routing prolif-340 

erative variation through its dedicated circular encoder, reconstructing the continuous topology 341 

of the cell cycle on  𝑢  

> cycle

(cell -cycle phase  aF1=0.87,  LISI=0.00 ; cell state  aF1=0. 32 ,342

the macroscopic state encoder to recover an unconfounded CD8+ T -cell differentiation trajec-344 

tory on  𝑢 state  (cell state  aF1=0.97,  LISI=0.02;  cell -cycle phase  aF1=0. 53 , LISI=0. 29 ; Extended 345 

Data Fig. 3b, c ). By contrast, linear empirical Bayes batch regression in C OM BAT  overcor-346 

rected for covariate variation and shattered the developmental continuum, whereas baseline 347 

embeddings from deep generative models including MoCAVI and MrVI remained dominated 348 

by cell -cycle variation ( Extended Data Fig. 3b, c ). Consistent with our simulation benchmarks 349

ogy of MoCAVI  𝑏𝑎𝑠𝑒  and  MrVI  𝑢  (Extended Data Fig. 3b ), confirming that upstream se-351 

mantic filtration is required to recover unconfounded developmental trajectories. 352 

PertVI ’s Split -FiLM transition also overcame a major failure mode of multi -condition pertur-353 

bation modeling when all 62 drugs were supplied as target covariates ( Extended Data Fig. 3b, 354 

c, “Perturbation ”). Whereas supervised LDA and  COMBAT  conflated  compound  responses 355 

with baseline state identity, MrVI failed to resolve  individual  compound effects  except 356 

Thapsigargin  (perturbation  aF1= 0.04 , LISI= 1.09 ), likely  because  its  high -magnitude  response 357 

monopolized the variance of shared sample -aware latent space.  MoCAVI , on the other hand, 358 

effectively captures dominant perturbat ion effects  upon centroid aggregation  (perturbation 359 

aF1=0. 21 , LISI=0. 93 ); however,  it suffers from a critical control -memorization artifact in im-360 

balanced screens . Compared to all the benchmarked approaches,  PertVI  successfully  isolat ed 361 

individual pharmacological effects in  𝑧  

> cycle

(perturbation  aF1=0.45,  LISI=0.54 ) and  𝑧 state  (per-362

clustering  and control -memorization  artifacts ( Extended Data Fig. 3b, c ). We  hence  used this 364 

disentangled causal structure to address three central  biological  questions across the screen: (1) 365

Continuous cell -cycle reconstruction and lineage -selective proliferation remodeling on  𝑢 cycle .370

PertVI disentangles cell -cycle dynamics, activation states, and context -specific genetic 611 

regulators in primary human  CD4+  T cells 612 

To demonstrate that PertVI generalizes to multi -condition screens, we applied it to a pilot da-613 

taset extracted from a recent genome -scale CRISPRi (ZIM3 -KRAB -dCas9) Perturb -seq atlas 614 

of primary human  CD4+  T cells 15  (Fig. 4a ). The parent atlas targeted 12,779 expressed genes 615 

(26,504 sgRNAs including 5% non -targeting controls) across 21.99 million single -guide pri-616 

mary human  CD4+  T cells across four healthy donors (D1 –D4) and three T -cell receptor (TCR; 617 

anti -CD3/CD28/CD2) stimulation conditions: resting baseline (Rest, unstimulated), acute ac-618 

tivation (Stim 8hr, 8h post -stimulation), and late effector maturation (Stim 48hr, 48h post -stim-619 

ulation), defining 111 co -functional perturbation clusters across conditions. Across this multi -620 

condition screen, single -cell transcriptomes reflect the combined influence of inter -donor bio-621 

logical variation (D1 –D4), chronological activation -state transitions (Rest to Stim 8hr and Stim 622 

48hr), rapid mitotic cell -cycle entry during 48h blastogenesis, and stage -dependent genetic per-623 

turbations. To benchmark PertVI across these coexisting sources of variation as a proof of 624 

principle, we constructed a stratified pilot dataset of 120,545 high -quality single -guide cells 625 

(Rest: 42,344 cells; Stim 8hr: 40,272 cells; Stim 48hr: 37,929 cells across D1 –D4) profiled 626 

across 10,004 expressed genes (the top 10,000 donor -aware highly variable genes unioned with 627 

94 canonical cell -cycle markers 18 ; Fig. 4a  and  Extended Data Fig. 8a ). Specifically, the pilot 628 

dataset includes 61 curated T -cell activation regulators (77,003 cells) drawn from the original 629 

study ’s T -cell activation clusters (Clusters 21, 22, and 99) and stratified by their active 630 

timepoints into early (active at 8h in Cluster 21,  𝑛  = 12 ; 19,248 cells), general (shared across 631 

8h in Clusters 21 and 22 and 48h in Cluster 99,  𝑛  = 29 ; 34,081 cells), and late (active at 48h 632 

in Cluster 99,  𝑛  = 20 ; 23,674 cells) stages across seven functional categories ( See  Methods 633 

6.1 ). Alongside the annotated active regulators, we also designed two sets of controls: 20 non -634 

essential control genes (NCGs; 37,518 cells) randomly sampled from perturbed genes strictly 635 

outside all co -functional clusters to serve as empirical null, and 6,024 uniformly sampled non -636 

targeting control (NTC) cells (5.00% of the dataset; 502 per donor -condition pool, 2,008 per 637 

condition, and 1,506 per donor) matching the parent library ’s 5% NTC proportion to anchor 638 

the unperturbed  baseline . Training PertVI over 400 epochs with donor batch conditioning and 639 

perturbation Feature -wise Linear Modulation converged smoothly across ELBO, reconstruc-640 

tion loss, and KL divergence ( Extended Data Fig. 8b ; Methods ). Comparing uncorrected ex-641 

pression with PertVI ’s six latent subspaces ( 𝑢 cycle , 𝑢 state , 𝑧 cycle , 𝑧 state , 𝑣 ood , 𝑧 final ) confirmed clean 642 

architectural disentanglement ( Fig. 4b  and  Extended Data Fig. 8c ). 643

PertVI eliminates mitotic confounding and remains SOTA integration performance.  Because 771 

each perturbation cohort in this screen is distributed across four donors, standard donor -pooled 772 

pseudobulk differential expression is vulnerable to small -sample dispersion noise. To test 773 

whether PertVI preserves state -of -the -art integration and denoising performance, we trained a 774 

dimensionality -matched, unconstrained 30 -dimensional scVI model 30  (30D scVI), which was 775 

used in the original study for single -cell embedding ( Extended Data Fig. 11a ; see  Methods ). 776 

We then compared five methods by benchmarking cross -donor split -half reproducibility (Pear-777 

son  𝑟 between Donors D1+D2 and Donors D3+D4 for all 61 target regulators across conditions; 778 

Extended Data Fig. 11b ). Single -cell generative pooling in both PertVI ( 𝑟  = 0.52 /0.73 /0.79 779 

across Rest / Stim 8hr / Stim 48hr) and 30D scVI ( 0.45 /0.72 /0.80 ) increased cross -donor 780 

reproducibility 3.6 - to 4.2 -fold over the donor -pooled PyDESeq2 approach used by the original 781 

study ( 0.13 /0.20 /0.19 ); meanwhile, PertVI  substantially outperformed raw single -cell obser-782 

vational differential expression (Raw Obs -DE;  0.20 /0.37 /0.48 ) and a donor -blocked pseudo-783 

bulk generalized linear model (Donor PB -GLM;  0.16 /0.40 /0.42 ; Extended Data Fig. 11b ). 784 

However, although both 30D scVI and PertVI harmonized inter -donor variation across D1 –D4, 785 

the undivided latent bottleneck of 30D scVI allowed high -amplitude mitotic variance to frac-786 

ture the Stim 8hr and Stim 48hr condition islands into phase -segregated G1, S, and G2/M sub -787 

lobes ( Extended Data Fig. 11a ), whereas PertVI isolated cell -cycle progression into  𝑢 cycle  to 788 

preserve a phase -homogenized activation manifold in  𝑢 state  (Fig. 4b  and  Extended Data Fig. 789 

8c ). 790 

To evaluate how this mitotic sub -lobe fracturing impacts perturbation inference, we projected 791 

the 30D scVI latent space onto the Chronological Activation Pseudotime (CPT) state axis ( Ex-792

Methods ). Inside both the Stim 8hr and Stim 48hr islands, the  scVI state and cycle gradient 794 

vectors aligned in nearly parallel directions ( Extended Data Fig. 11c, d ). Consequently, across 795 

all 81 perturbations, scVI state and cycle shifts exhibited strong collinearity at pre -mitotic Stim 796 

8hr ( 𝑟  = 0.60 ; Fig. 4j , left), pulling cytoskeletal and DNA -damage checkpoints ( WAS , RNF8 )797 

into false activation arrest alongside proximal TCR and calcium regulators ( ZAP70 , ORAI1 ,798 

ATP2A2 ), whereas PertVI reduced 8h collinearity ( 𝑟  = 0.40 ; Fig. 4j , right) and buffered  WAS 799 

and  RNF8  near zero on  𝑢 state  . At rapidly dividing Stim 48hr, scVI state and cycle shifts 800

collapsed onto an entangled diagonal ( 𝑟  = 0.62 ; Fig. 4k , left) that swept pure DNA -repair 801

generative integration while preventing proliferative arrest from corrupting the inferred direc-814 

tionality and biological identity of T -cell activation regulators. 815 

> 816

DISCUSSION 817 

Single -cell perturbation screens have rapidly expanded from uniform, steady -state cell lines 6

> 818

into dynamic, multi -condition biological systems such as differentiating and proliferating pri-819 

mary human lymphocytes 12,13,15 . Existing linear, contrastive, and latent -shift frameworks were 820 

predominantly developed for steady -state contexts where a static cellular baseline holds 32,35,37 .821 

Under those static conditions or strong perturbation regimes, approaches such as  CPA 56 , Con-822 

trastiveVI 32 , MrVI 41 , and MoCAVI 42  effectively isolate treatment effects against a single back-823 

ground representation. As perturbation screens transition into dynamic systems, however, lo-824 

calized intervention signals are overshadowed by macroscopic lineage progression, periodic 825 

cell -cycle oscillations, and donor heterogeneity that covary and share pleiotropic target genes. 826 

Because existing models either compress the unperturbed baseline into a single, undivided 827 

background space or distribute covarying state signals across unsupervised splits without axis 828 

specificity, variance entanglement becomes the primary bottleneck in dynamic screens.  In par-829 

ticular, o ur ground -truth simulation study ( Fig. 2 ) revealed that  even in an idealized log -linear 830 

additive setting devoid of high -order nonlinear epistasis, covarying cell -cycle and differentia-831 

tion programs  significantly  mask weak, target -overlapping perturbations when compressed into 832 

an undivided baseline.  In addition to the simulation, t he chemical screen in differentiating and 833 

actively dividing primary human  CD8+  T cells 63  (Fig. 3 ) represents a typical real -world case 834 

of this entanglement.  We found that w hen the cellular baseline is encoded within a  unified 835 

space, high -amplitude mitotic oscillations dominate the baseline embeddings of MrVI and 836 

MoCAVI, obscuring the continuous naive -to -effector differentiation trajectory while extreme 837 

stressors (such as Thapsigargin in MrVI) or class -imbalance control -memorization artifacts 838 

(MoCAVI) distort their  sample covariate or  perturbation spaces.  Our approach,  PertVI , re-839 

solves this entanglement across both simulated and primary dynamic screens by explicitly sep-840 

arating periodic cell division ( 𝑢 cycle ) from cellular state ( 𝑢 state ) prior to perturbation condition-841 

ing, recovering an unconfounded developmental trajectory alongside sensitive, condition -spe-842 

cific treatment responses. 843 

Transitioning from steady -state cell lines to dynamic primary systems also requires rethinking 844 

the geometric assumptions used to represent perturbation responses. Current generative 31,56 

> 845

and optimal -transport 29  frameworks operate under a closed, uni -manifold assumption, model-846 

ing every chemical or genetic intervention as a trajectory shift within a single latent coordinate 847 

space. In dynamic cellular systems, however, interventions simultaneously modulate 848 

> preprint (which was not certified by peer review) is the author/funder. All rights reserved. No reuse allowed without permission. The copyright holder for this this version posted November 10, 2026. ;https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.11.07.765083 doi: bioRxiv preprint 19

physiological trajectories and trigger acute off -manifold  toxicity . PertVI replaces this closed, 849 

uni -manifold view with an open, multi -manifold architecture ( Fig. 1 ). Specifically, affine Split -850 

FiLM conditioning 61  captures on -manifold physiological transitions along perturbation -blind 851 

baseline anchors ( 𝑢 cycle  → 𝑧 cycle  and  𝑢 state  → 𝑧 state ), while unaccommodated nonlinear variance 852 

routes into an origin -anchored anomaly subspace ( 𝑣 ood  ) interrogated via the Marginalized 853 

Counterfactual Operator (MCO). In differentiating primary human  CD8+  T cells, this multi -854 

manifold decomposition reveals two widely neglected relationships ( Fig. 3 ). First, within the 855 

on -manifold regime, phenotypic fate redirection  can be  largely decoupled from global tran-856 

scriptional magnitude and governed instead by localized, stage -conditioned impulses. Conse-857 

quently, low -magnitude lineage -gating catalysts such as CGP -60474, TWS -119, and decitabine 858 

redirect differentiation by concentrating their transcriptional impulse at narrow developmental 859 

transition windows. Conversely, high -magnitude compounds such as calcitriol, pomalidomide, 860 

and Belinostat induce broad  transcriptomic  programs that remain homeostatically buffered 861 

along  𝑢 state  . Second, neither on -manifold trajectory shifts nor off -manifold stress distances 862 

alone  is sufficient  to prioritize therapeutic immunomodulators. Joint evaluation of on -manifold 863 

shifting  and off -manifold  responses  separates genuine memory -precursor adjuvants ( e.g. , de-864 

citabine, TWS -119) from toxic phenocopies ( e.g.,  Serdemetan  and Wortmannin). Whereas 865 

genuine adjuvants remodel state occupancy cleanly on -manifold,  toxic phenocopies  mimic 866 

IL7R + memory accumulation on  𝑢 state  while triggering p53 -mediated bioenergetic collapse or 867 

apoptosis in  𝑣 ood .868 

Beyond  isolating on -manifold shifting and off -manifold response , structuring deep architec-869 

tures around variance disentanglement unlocks the full resolution of single -cell transcriptomics 870 

to dissect  context -specific mechanisms and reconstruct non -observational cellular phenotypes 871 

in silico  when physical sorting is unavailable. In unsorted primary human  CD8+  T cells ex-872 

posed to small -molecule perturbations ( Fig. 3 ), standard discrete gene -set scoring and bulk 873 

differential expression obscure sub -phase and stage -specific drug mechanisms. By projecting 874 

cells onto continuous angular coordinates on  𝑢 cycle , PertVI resolves a late -S replication crisis 875 

under camptothecin and Serdemetan that discrete phase -score crossover misclassifies as mi-876 

totic arrest. Simultaneously, along the deconfounded differentiation trajectory ( 𝑧 state ), state -877 

conditioned  counterfactual analysis  reveals that memory -shifting compounds  may influence T 878 

cell fate switch  via  restraining ribosomal RNA maturation 102,103 , E2F/DREAM proliferation 879 

programs 104 , and the  CTCF  chromatin -looping regulon within late  differentiation stage 105,106 .880 

In pooled genetic screens , the capacity to reconstruct non -observational phenotypes is  also  par-881 

ticularly valuable ( Fig. 4 ). Classical pooled CRISPR screens rely on fluorescence -activated 882 

cell sorting of a single surface receptor, cytokine, or proliferation dye to obtain a signed phe-883 

notypic readout at the expense of transcriptomic breadth. Conversely, droplet - and probe -based 884 

Perturb -seq screens capture transduced cells across  conditions  without physical marker sorting, 885 

making  standard  approaches  hard to map  regulators into  directional regulatory modules  (e.g., 886 

Cluster 99 in stimulated  CD4+  T cells). By projecting unsorted  CD4+  T cells onto  disentangled 887 

activation ( 𝑢 state ) and mitotic ( 𝑢 cycle ) manifolds, PertVI reconstructs  a continuous , in silico  sort-888 

ing ax is  directly from raw transcriptomes. These deconfounded coordinates recover chrono-889 

logical stage hierarchies (Early, General, and Late activation gates), resolve unsigned clusters 890 

into signed directional trajectories that separate positive TCR signalosome promoters from 891 

tonic checkpoint brakes, and uncouple cell -cycle checkpoint arrest from  canonical  immuno-892 

logical activation.  Grounded by literature evidence across both screens, our downstream bio-893 

logical investigation demonstrates that through structural variance disentanglement, a compu-894 

tational engine can recapitulate in -depth regulatory relationships directly from unsorted single -895 

cell data, establishing a deconfounded foundation that can be extended further by integrating 896 

formal statistical causal inference 114 .897 

> preprint (which was not certified by peer review) is the author/funder. All rights reserved. No reuse allowed without permission. The copyright holder for this this version posted November 10, 2026. ;https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.11.07.765083 doi: bioRxiv preprint 20

Importantly, b eyond its methodological framework, our study  also  illustrates how re -examin-898 

ing high -throughput screens through a disentangled multi -manifold architecture can uncover 899 

rich, previously neglected biological landscapes. Whereas the original study that generated the 900 

primary human  CD8+ T cell  chemical screen used the dataset primarily to train active -learning 901 

models for compound prioritization across  cancer  cell lines and primary cohorts 63 , applying 902 

PertVI to this screen reveals a comprehensive immuno -oncology landscape of how anti -cancer 903 

agents  interact with  primary human  CD8+ T -cell proliferation, differentiation, and survival 904 

(Fig. 3 ). On the mitotic manifold ( 𝑢 cycle ), cross -referencing primary T -cell sub -phase arrest 905 

profiles against tumor cell -line arrest phenotypes defines four immuno -oncology proliferation 906 

classes, separating conserved anti -proliferati on  agents  (such as mTOR  inhibitors and  JAK  in-907 

hibitors) from tumor -active, T -cell -sparing oncology drugs including  MEK inhibitors, RTK 908 

inhibitors  and several epigenetic modulators . Along with the differentiation trajectory ( 𝑢 state ), 909 

PertVI  further  resolves  how anti -cancer agents can interact with effector T cell differentiation 910 

and concludes three major archetypes as  naive -arrest, effector -promoting, and memory -shifting. 911 

Integrating proliferation,  differentiation,  and off -manifold toxicity , PertVI reveals a compre-912 

hensive landscape of heterogenous  pharmacological interaction between anti -cancer drugs and 913 

CD8+ T cell effector differentiation  at a granularity that conventional approaches alone can 914 

hardly achieve. 915 

Together, our findings point to a broader methodological transition in functional genomics. As 916 

experimental platforms increasingly shift toward dynamic, multi -condition perturbation 917 

screens, the intertwined variation of proliferation, lineage progression, and cellular stress ex-918 

ceeds the assumptions of analytical frameworks originally designed for steady -state baselines. 919 

Advancing what computational tools can achieve in mechanistic dissection therefore calls for 920 

deep generative architectures that evolve in tandem with multi -condition experimental designs, 921 

providing a principled foundation to uncover the biological and therapeutic insights that mod-922 

ern perturbation screens are designed to  reveal .923 

> 924

LIMITATIONS OF CURRENT STUDY 925 

PertVI is designed around a defined set of architectural assumptions, and we note three scope -926 

specific constraints that users should consider when applying the model. 927 

First, the current Split -FiLM transition module indexes each cell through a single categorical 928 

perturbation identifier. While this formulation matches standard low -multiplicity -of -infection 929 

(MOI) CRISPR screens in which each cell receives a single guide RNA, as well as single -agent 930 

chemical screens profiled at a fixed concentration, a single categorical lookup embedding can-931 

not decompose additive main effects from non -linear epistasis in high -MOI multi -guide trans-932 

ductions or multi -drug combinations, nor can it interpolate across continuous dose -response 933 

gradients. Extending the transition module to support compositional multi -perturbation embed-934 

dings and continuous dose covariates would generalize the framework to combinatorial and 935 

dose -resolved screens. 936 

Second, PertVI ’s baseline disentanglement relies on semi -supervised gene masking and multi -937 

objective regularization while compressing all non -mitotic physiological variation into a single 938 

continuous state manifold ( 𝑢 state ). Specifically, isolating periodic division ( 𝑢 cycle ) from  𝑢 state 939 

and the anomaly subspace ( 𝑣 ood ) depends on a user -supplied cell -cycle marker mask ( 𝑀 cycle ) in 940 

the bifurcated decoder. Consequently, separation quality is sensitive to marker -set complete-941 

ness and specificity in cell types with atypical cycling programs . Besides,  the multi -encoder 942 

objective introduces higher training costs alongside hyperparameter sensitivity . Insufficient re-943 

pulsion  will likely cause  residual signal leakage between subspaces, whereas overly aggressive 944 

> preprint (which was not certified by peer review) is the author/funder. All rights reserved. No reuse allowed without permission. The copyright holder for this this version posted November 10, 2026. ;https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.11.07.765083 doi: bioRxiv preprint 21

regularization can attenuate genuine biological covariation. Moreover, while acute perturba-945 

tion -induced  off -manifold stress  routes into  𝑣 ood , all remaining on -manifold baseline variation 946 

is modeled as a single continuous  𝑢 state  representation; when multiple endogenous physiologi-947 

cal programs unfold concurrently, such as simultaneous branching along distinct differentiation 948 

lineages or parallel cytokine polarization trajectories, they remain jointly encoded within  𝑢 state .949 

Automating penalty calibration via held -out validation metrics and factorizing  𝑢 state  into prior -950 

guided baseline sub -manifolds would broaden interpretability in  systems  with higher -order 951 

baseline complexity. 952 

Third, although PertVI deploys counterfactual pushforward operators to evaluate perturbation 953 

responses while holding baseline coordinates constant, structural variance disentanglement 954 

within a variational autoencoder is distinct from formal statistical causal inference 114 . By re-955 

moving observational confounders including technical batch shifts, donor compositional im-956 

balance, mitotic phase collinearity, and off -manifold cytotoxicity, Split -FiLM parameterizes a 957 

conditional affine push -forward that captures the net downstream transcriptional displacement 958 

of an intervention relative to baseline anchors. However, this affine transition does not establish 959 

directed acyclic graph structure identifiability, invariant causal prediction across environ-960 

ments 114 , or Pearl ’s do -calculus over gene -regulatory networks 115 , and therefore does not re-961 

solve the directed gene -by -gene cascade connecting a primary molecular target to  terminal 962 

phenotype . Integrati on of  causal graph discovery with disentangled architectures  will  therefore 963 

be the natural future direction to  achieve the complete causal disentanglement .964 

965 

METHODS 966 

1.  PertVI (Disentangled  Variational  Inference  for Perturbation Response) 967 

1.1  Problem Formulation 968 

Let  𝑥 𝑛  ∈ ℤ≥0 

> 𝐺

denote the raw UMI count vector for cell  𝑛  across  𝐺  genes, where  𝑛  ∈ {1, … , 𝑁 }.969 

Each cell is associated with a perturbation identifier  𝑠 𝑛 , encoding drug identity or guide RNA 970 

identity , and a technical covariate vector  𝑏 𝑛  encoding batch membership or biological replicate. 971 

PertVI factorizes the transcriptome of cell  𝑛  as a composition of three causally disjoint latent 972 

variables: a low -dimensional cell -cycle anchor  𝑢 𝑐𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒 ,𝑛  , a macroscopic biological state an-973 

chor  𝑢 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒 ,𝑛 , and an out -of -distribution (OOD) novelty variable  𝑣 𝑜𝑜𝑑 ,𝑛 . The perturbation iden-974 

tifier  𝑠 𝑛  enters the model exclusively through a post -inference affine transition module, ensur-975 

ing that all encoder computations remain strictly blind to the perturbation label during inference. 976 

1.2  Generative Model 977 

Priors.  All three latent variables are assigned standard Gaussian priors: 978 

𝑢 𝑐𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒 ,𝑛  ∼ 𝒩  (0, 𝐼 𝑑 𝑐𝑦𝑐  ) , 𝑢 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒 ,𝑛  ∼ 𝒩 (0, 𝐼 𝑑 𝑠𝑡  ), 𝑣 𝑜𝑜𝑑 ,𝑛  ∼ 𝒩 (0, 𝐼 𝑑 𝑜𝑜𝑑  )979 

where  𝑑 𝑐𝑦𝑐  ∈ {2, 3}, 𝑑 𝑠𝑡  ≈ 30, and  𝑑 𝑜𝑜𝑑  ≈ 10 denote the respective latent dimensionalities. 980 

The low dimensionality of  𝑢 𝑐𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒  serves as a topological bottleneck: by constraining periodic 981 

transcriptional variance to a two - or three -dimensional manifold, the encoder is compelled to 982 

represent the continuous ring topology of the cell cycle without imposing an explicit topologi-983 

cal prior. 984 

Decoder.  Gene expression is reconstructed through three structurally isolated decoder heads. 985

> Δ𝑧 𝑛 (transcriptomic momentum )

1038 

The momentum vector  Δ𝑧 𝑛  encodes both the direction and magnitude of the perturbation -in-1039 

duced displacement along the baseline biological manifold. Because the FiLM operator is re-1040 

stricted to element -wise affine transformations along existing latent axes, it cannot represent 1041 

topological ruptures or non -linear deviations from the baseline manifold. Perturbation -induced 1042 

variance that exceeds this linear expressivity , such as cytotoxic endoplasmic reticulum stress 1043 

or apoptotic signaling , is rejected from the FiLM mechanism and routes instead into the OOD 1044 

novelty variable  𝑣 𝑜𝑜𝑑 ,𝑛 , which bypasses the transition module entirely. The full aggregated 1045 

representation passed to the main decoder is: 1046 

𝑧 𝑓𝑖𝑛𝑎𝑙 ,𝑛  = [𝑧 𝑐𝑦𝑐𝑙𝑒 ,𝑛 ; 𝑧 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒 ,𝑛 ; 𝑣 𝑜𝑜𝑑 ,𝑛 ]1047

2.  Post -training Counterfactual Analyses 1099 

Overall, PertVI enables three major downstream  counterfactual  analyses, each of which queries 1100 

the trained main head by contrasting perturbed and control latent inputs while holding nuisance 1101 

inputs fixed. The first, a global path -integrated momentum score  (2.1) , integrates the effect of 1102 

each perturbation ’s learned FiLM modulation along a path through basal state space, such as 1103 

an activation or differentiation trajectory. It summarizes the average transcriptional drive of the 1104 

perturbation across that continuum and, optionally, how strongly the drive varies along the path. 1105 

The second, a counterfactual causal score  (2.2) , asks how the trained decoder predicts that 1106 

control cells of the same condition would respond if they were shifted by the perturbation ’s1107 

empirical displacement in basal state space. This is evaluated either with the perturbation ’s1108 

own FiLM modulation or with the control modulation, giving condition -resolved, model -based 1109 

estimates of perturbation effects at the gene and pathway level. The third, a marginalized coun-1110 

terfactual operator  (2.3) , first screens perturbations by how far their cells are displaced in the 1111 

OOD subspace. It then extracts gene -level OOD signatures by substituting each perturbation ’s1112 

OOD centroid into the decoder across a single fixed control reference population, so that the 1113 

perturbation ’s own state distribution does not enter the evaluation. Together, these modules 1114 

separate on -manifold state transitions from off -manifold responses,  and the detailed derivation 1115 

of each module is presented below .1116

CCS is a model -based counterfactual. It asks how, according to the trained decoder, the main -1161

manifold under control regulatory rules, whereas Coupled -CCS adds the perturbation ’s direct 1165 

FiLM remodeling. 1166

3.  Synthetic  Multi -covariate  Perturb -seq  Data  Simulation 1182 

We generated a synthetic  multi -covariate  single -cell RNA -seq dataset of 10,000 cells and 100 1183 

genes to benchmark  the  latent -factor disentanglement methods under controlled ground truth. 1184 

The simulation was designed to simultaneously embed four sources of variation  – cell differ-1185 

entiation state, cell -cycle progression, sequencing batch, and transcriptional perturbation  – in1186 

a hierarchical variance structure ( State > Cell Cycle  (CC)  > Batch > Perturbation ), mimicking 1187 

the relative magnitudes observed in real Perturb -seq experiments. 1188 

Cell Metadata.  Each cell was assigned a continuous cell -cycle pseudotime coordinate  𝜑  drawn 1189 

uniformly from  [0,3) and a continuous differentiation pseudotime coordinate  τ drawn uni-1190 

formly from  0,2. Cells were labelled with a discrete cell -cycle phase (G1:  𝜑  ∈ [0,1), S:  𝜑  ∈1191 

[1,2), G2M:  𝜑  ∈ [2,3)), yielding approximately equal phase proportions (G1: 33.8%, S: 33.9%, 1192 

G2M: 32.3%). From  𝜏 , cells were assigned to three differentiation states via equal -width inter-1193 

vals (State1:  𝜏  < 2/3, State2:  𝜏  ∈ [2/3,4/3), State3:  𝜏  ≥ 4/3). Five experimental conditions 1194 

(one non -targeting control, four perturbations) were represented with 2,000 cells each. Batch 1195 

assignment was performed by stratified randomization within each of the 45 cross -tabulated 1196 

groups of perturbation × CC phase × state, ensuring balanced representation across two batches. 1197 

Gene layout.  Of the 100 simulated genes, 30 were designated cell -cycle markers (10 G1 -phase 1198 

markers: genes 050 –059; 10 S -phase markers: genes 000 –009; 10 G2M markers: genes 010 –1199 

019), 30 were differentiation state markers (10 per state, genes 020 –049), and 40 were back-1200 

ground genes (genes 060 –099). The G1 marker set was a key design feature introduced to 1201 

prevent G1 -phase cells from collapsing to the expression mean in the absence of phase -specific 1202 

signal. 1203 

Cell -cycle effect model.  Cell -cycle marker expression was modelled using a symmetric half -1204 

sinusoidal bump function applied to all three marker groups. Each bump was parameterized by 1205 

an onset position placed at the midpoint of the preceding phase zone, a width of 1.5 pseudotime 1206 

units (equal to half the ring circumference), and a peak log fold change (LFC) of 3.5. Formally, 1207 

for a cell at pseudotime  𝜑 , the CC contribution to log -expression for marker group  k was: 1208 

𝜓 ₖ = 𝑚𝑜𝑑 (𝜑  − 𝜑 𝑜𝑛𝑠𝑒𝑡 ,𝑘 , 3.0)1209 

𝛼 𝐶𝐶 , 𝑘 (𝜑 ) = 𝐿𝐹 𝐶 𝐶𝐶  × 𝑠𝑖𝑛 (𝜋  𝜓 ₖ/1.5) if  𝜓 ₖ < 1.5, else  01210 

with onset positions  𝜑 𝑜𝑛𝑠𝑒𝑡 ,𝐺 1 = 2.5, 𝜑 𝑜𝑛𝑠𝑒𝑡 ,𝑆  = 0.5, and  𝜑 𝑜𝑛𝑠𝑒𝑡 ,𝐺 2𝑀  = 1.5. This design cre-1211 

ates a 0.5 -unit overlap between each adjacent pair of phases (  𝐺 1 ∩ 𝑆 : 𝜑  ∈ [0.5,1.0) ; 𝑆  ∩1212 

𝐺 2𝑀 : 𝜑  ∈ [1.5,2.0); 𝐺 2𝑀  ∩ 𝐺 1: 𝜑  ∈ [2.5,3.0)), during which two marker sets are simultane-1213 

ously expressed at intermediate levels, reflecting the gradual transitions observed in biological 1214 

> preprint (which was not certified by peer review) is the author/funder. All rights reserved. No reuse allowed without permission. The copyright holder for this this version posted November 10, 2026. ;https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.11.07.765083 doi: bioRxiv preprint 28

cell -cycle progression. Phase marker sets are mutually exclusive at their respective peaks (each 1215 

marker is exactly zero at the peaks of all other markers). The symmetric design ensures that all 1216 

three phase transitions are equally represented in the expression manifold, producing a near -1217 

equilateral triangular arrangement of phase centroids in CC -gene PCA space (pairwise centroid 1218 

distances: G1 ↔S = 5.5 8, S ↔G2M = 5. 142, G2M ↔G1 =  5.08 ; symmetry ratio = 1.1 0). 1219 

Differentiation state effect model.  Differentiation state was encoded using tent (triangular hat) 1220 

basis functions  𝑤 ₁(𝜏 ), 𝑤 ₂(𝜏 ), 𝑤 ₃(𝜏 ) that form a partition of unity over  𝜏  ∈ [0,2]. State marker 1221 

genes received a log -mean contribution  𝛽𝑘 (𝜏 ) = 𝛴ⱼ𝐴𝑘𝑗  · 𝑤ⱼ (𝜏 ), where the coefficient matrix 1222 

was  𝐴  = [[+5, −1, −3], [−3, +5, −3], [−3, −1, +5]] (rows: states 1–3; columns: basis 1223 

weights  𝑤 ₁–𝑤 ₃). The peak LFC of +5.0 was selected to achieve Cell State  𝜂 ² > 1.5 × Cell Cy-1224 

cle  𝜂 ².1225 

Cross -covariate coupling.  To reflect the biological reality that state and CC axes are not fully 1226 

orthogonal, a small cross -coupling term was included: state marker genes received an addi-1227 

tional CC -dependent log -mean contribution weighted by a gene -specific coupling coefficient 1228 

drawn from  𝐶𝑙𝑖𝑝 (𝒩 (0.35 ,0.05 ), 0.2,0.5), and CC marker genes received a state -dependent 1229 

contribution weighted by  𝐶𝑙𝑖𝑝 (𝒩 (0.50 ,0.05 ), 0.3,0.7).1230 

Batch effects.  A per -gene batch shift  𝛿 ᵍ was drawn from  𝒩 (0,0.45 ). Batch -0 cells received 1231 

+𝛿 ᵍ and Batch -1 cells received  −𝛿 ᵍ for each gene, creating symmetric batch separation with 1232 

no net effect on population mean. Library sizes were drawn from batch -specific lognormal 1233 

distributions (Batch -0: mean ≈ 5,800 UMIs; Batch -1: mean ≈ 6,800 UMIs) and used to scale 1234 

expected total counts per cell via a cell -specific normali zation constant. 1235 

Perturbation effects.  Perturbation  𝐴  targeted 50 genes (20 sampled from the CC/state gene pool, 1236 

30 from the background pool) with effect sizes drawn from  𝒩 (0,0.8). Perturbation  𝐵  was con-1237 

structed to be highly correlated with A: 45 of the 50  𝐴 -targeted genes were retained (90% over-1238 

lap) with  𝐵 -specific effects defined as  0.85  × 𝛾 𝐴  + 𝒩 (0,0.10 ), yielding a Pearson correlation 1239 

𝑟 (𝛾 𝐴 , 𝛾 𝐵 ) = 0.993 . Perturbation  𝐶  targeted 18 genes drawn exclusively from genes not tar-1240 

geted by either  𝐴  or  𝐵  (0% overlap with  𝐴  ∪ 𝐵 ), representing an orthogonal perturbation. Per-1241 

turbation  𝐷  added negligible genome -wide noise ( 𝒩 (0,0.05 ) across all genes) to serve as a 1242 

near -null control. The non -targeting control (NT) carried no perturbation effect. 1243 

Count generation.  Final expected log -means were assembled as: 1244 

𝑙𝑜𝑔𝜇 ᵢᵍ  = 𝑙𝑜𝑔𝜇 ₀ᵍ  + 𝛼 𝐶𝐶 ᵢᵍ + 𝛽 𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑡𝑒 ᵢᵍ + 𝛾 𝑝𝑒𝑟𝑡 ᵢᵍ + 𝜆 𝑖𝑛𝑡𝑒𝑟𝑎𝑐𝑡𝑖𝑜𝑛 ᵢᵍ + 𝛿 𝑏𝑎𝑡𝑐 ℎᵢ ᵍ + 𝑐 𝑙𝑖𝑏 ᵢ1245 

where  𝑐 𝑙𝑖𝑏 ᵢ is a cell -specific scaling constant that normalises expected total counts to the sam-1246 

pled library size. Counts were sampled from a Zero -Inflated Negative Binomial (ZINB) model: 1247 

Poisson rates  λᵢᵍ were first sampled from a  Γ(𝑟 ᵍ, 𝜇 ᵢᵍ /𝑟 ᵍ) distribution (where  𝑟 ᵍ = 1/𝐵𝐶𝑉 ² en-1248 

codes gene -level overdispersion), raw counts were drawn from  𝑃𝑜𝑖𝑠𝑠𝑜𝑛 (𝜆 ᵢᵍ ), and a gene - and 1249 

expression -level -dependent dropout probability  𝜋  = 𝑠𝑖𝑔𝑚𝑜𝑖𝑑 (−1.5 − 0.8𝑙𝑜𝑔 (𝜇  + 0.1)) was 1250 

applied as a  𝐵𝑒𝑟𝑛𝑜𝑢𝑙𝑙𝑖  mask to introduce excess zeros. The resulting dataset had 41.2% spar-1251 

sity and a mean of 6,263 UMIs per cell. 1252 

Variance decomposition.  The relative contributions of each biological and technical covariate 1253 

to transcriptome -wide variance were quantified using the mean per -gene  𝜂 ² computed on 1254 

𝑙𝑜𝑔 1𝑝 -normalized counts. The final variance hierarchy  follows  Cell State  (19.6%) >  Cell Cycle 1255 

(10.9%) >  Batch  (4.6%) >  Perturbation  (3.6%), with a State -to -Cell Cycle ratio of 1.81×  to 1256 

achieve the situation where  cell state should be the dominant source of variation while cell 1257 

cycle remains clearly distinguishable above batch  (Fig. 2a  and  Extended Data Fig. 1 ). All 1258 

simulations were performed in Python 3.11  with a fixed random seed  of  42 for reproducibility. 1259

4.  Benchmark 1260 

4.1  Shared Training Protocol 1261 

To ensure comparability across models, a common training configuration was enforced for all 1262 

benchmarked methods  except for two standard approaches (COMBAT and LDA) . All model -1263 

based methods were evaluated on the complete  10 ,000  cells  × 100  genes  synthetic dataset, us-1264 

ing raw UMI counts for count -based probabilistic models and  ln (CP10K  + 1)-normalized ex-1265 

pression for continuous matrix methods. To evaluate generalization to unseen cells under a 1266 

data -sparse training regime ( 20%  combined training and validation vs.  80%  held -out test, 1267 

8,000  cells), the dataset was partitioned via stratified random splitting stratified jointly across 1268 

the  10  perturbation  × batch strata ( 5 perturbations  × 2 batches ), yielding a  2,000 -cell train-1269 

ing/validation pool ( 1,800  training and  200  validation cells for the  18% /2% /80%  three -way 1270 

split used by LinearSCVI, DrVI, ContrastiveVI, and MoCAVI, or  2,000  training and  8,000 1271 

validation/test cells passed via external  indexing for PertVI and MrVI). PertVI and MrVI were 1272 

trained for up to  2,000  epochs with early stopping (max_epochs = 2000, early_stopping = True, 1273 

converging at  755  and  225  epochs, respectively), whereas LinearSCVI, DrVI, ContrastiveVI, 1274 

and MoCAVI were trained for a fixed  200  epochs (max_epochs = 200, early_stopping = False), 1275 

using minibatch size batch_size = 512, the Adam optimizer with learning rate  10 −3, and two -1276 

layer 128 -unit multi -layer perceptron (MLP) encoders and decoders on a single NVIDIA Ge-1277 

Force RTX 5090 GPU (32 GB VRAM;  Extended Data Fig. 2a ). No model received ground -1278 

truth cell_cycle_phase, cc_pseudotime, cell_state, or diff_pseudotime annotations during train-1279 

ing. For uniform 2D visualization ( Fig. 2c ),  𝑘 -nearest -neighbor graphs (scanpy.pp.neighbors) 1280 

and Uniform Manifold Approximation and Projection (UMAP) embeddings (scanpy.tl.umap) 1281 

were computed from each learned latent representation in Scanpy 38 .1282 

4.2  Benchmarked  Approaches 1283 

Uncorrected.  For the uncorrected baseline representation, raw UMI counts were library -size 1284 

normalized, natural -log transformed (scanpy.pp.log1p), centered and scaled to unit variance 1285 

per gene, and reduced via PCA without batch or covariate adjustment, followed by 15 -nearest -1286 

neighbor graph construction and 2D UMAP projection ( Fig. 2c, d  and  Extended Data Fig. 1287 

2b ). 1288 

ComBat.  Parametric empirical Bayes batch adjustment (scanpy.pp.combat) was applied to the 1289 

ln (CP10K  + 1) -normalized expression matrix specifying sequencing batch (Batch0 versus 1290 

Batch1) as the batch key and perturbation as the preserved biological covariate, followed by 1291 

unit -variance scaling, 30 -component PCA, and 2D UMAP projection ( Fig. 2c, d  and  Extended 1292 

Data Fig. 2b ). 1293 

LDA.  Supervised multi -class Linear Discriminant Analysis was fitted on the top  30  uncor-1294 

rected principal components using the 5 -class perturbation identity as the supervision target 1295 

(test_size = 0.20, random_state = 42), projecting all  10 ,000  cells onto the canonical discrimi-1296 

nant axes prior to neighbor graph construction and 2D UMAP embedding ( Fig. 2c, d  and  Ex-1297 

tended Data Fig. 2b ). 1298 

LinearSCVI.  LinearSCVI 35  was configured with a 30 -dimensional latent space (n_latent = 30), 1299 

layer = "counts", batch_key = "batch", a non -linear MLP encoder (dropout_rate = 0.1), and a 1300 

linear factor -loading decoder under a Negative Binomial likelihood (gene_likelihood = "nb", 1301 

dispersion = "gene"), reconstructing log -normalized expression as  𝑥 ̂𝑖  = 𝑧 𝑖 𝐿 ⊤ + 𝑏 batch , where 1302 

𝐿  ∈ ℝ100 ×30  is the learned gene loading matrix (model.get_loadings()) and  𝑏 batch  is a batch -1303 

specific intercept ( Extended Data Fig. 2a ). No perturbation labels were supplied during train-1304 

ing. Analytical gene -level perturbation effect estimates ( Extended Data Fig. 2f, g ) were 1305

4.3  Downstream Analysis 1376 

Latent space disentanglement benchmarking.  Across all benchmarked representations (Uncor-1377 

rected, ComBat, LDA, LinearSCVI, MrVI  𝑢  and  𝑧 , DrVI at n_split_latent = 3 and n_split_la-1378

sources (batch [ 2 classes], cell_cycle_phase [ 3 classes], cell_state [ 3 classes], and perturbation 1382 

[5 classes]) using adversarial F1 ( aF1 ) and Local Inverse Simpson's Index ( LISI )116  (Fig. 2d 1383 

and  Extended Data Fig. 2b, d, e ).  Specifically,  aF1 was computed as the uncalibrated 5 -fold 1384 

stratified cross -validation (StratifiedKFold, n_splits = 5, shuffle = True, random_state = 42) 1385 

macro -averaged F1 score (f1_score, average = "macro") of a  𝑘  -nearest -neighbor classifier 1386 

(KNeighborsClassifier, n_neighbors = 10, weights = "uniform"). For local neighborhood mix-1387 

ing, per -cell  LISI  scores were computed across all cells using harmonypy.compute_lisi (per-1388 

plexity = 30) and summarized as  log 10 (median  LISI ). For the targeted DrVI split -latent exper-1389 

iments (n_split_latent = 3 with three 10 -dimensional blocks and n_split_latent = 5 with five 6 -1390 

dimensional blocks), each contiguous split block was evaluated independently via  aF1  and 1391 

LISI  across the four ground -truth covariates ( Extended Data Fig. 2d, e ). 1392 

Continuous cell -cycle ring and differentiation trajectory evaluation.  To evaluate whether each 1393 

method reconstructed the periodic ring topology of the cell cycle (G1 –S–G2M) separately from 1394 

the ordered differentiation continuum (State1 –State2 –State3), ground -truth continuous cell -cy-1395 

cle pseudotime ( 𝜑 𝑖  ∈ [0, 3)) and differentiation pseudotime ( 𝜏 𝑖  ∈ [0, 2]) were overlaid onto 1396 

each method ’s UMAP embeddings ( Fig. 2c ). In addition, diffusion -based pseudotime (Palan-1397 

tir 117  initialized from the State1 origin) was inferred on each model ’s baseline or state manifold 1398

and compared against ground -truth  𝜏 𝑖  via Spearman rank correlation ( Fig. 2e  and  Extended 1399

5.  Modeling on  CD8+ T Cell  Data 1420 

5.1  Data collection, subset, and preprocess 1421 

Data collection.  Single -cell RNA -sequencing (scRNA -seq) profiles from a high -throughput 1422 

chemical perturbation screen in primary human CD8+ T cells were obtained from  GSE306429. 1423 

In the experimental assay ( Fig. 3a ), purified peripheral blood mononuclear cell (PBMC) -de-1424 

rived naive CD8+ T cells from two healthy blood donors (Donor 1 and Donor 2, serving as two 1425 

biological replicates  𝑏 𝑛  ∈ {1, 2}) were pre -incubated for 24h with one of 62 structurally diverse 1426 

small -molecule compounds at  1𝜇 Mor with dimethyl sulfoxide (DMSO) vehicle control, fol-1427 

lowed by 72h of TCR stimulation  by  anti -CD3/CD28 -coated beads and  recombinant human 1428 

IL -2 to induce clonal proliferation and effector differentiation. The resulting cohort comprises 1429 

𝑁  = 62 ,207  quality -controlled single CD8+ T cells ( 34 ,548  cells from Donor 1 and  27 ,659 1430 

cells from Donor 2) spanning 63 experimental conditions:  6,472  unperturbed DMSO vehicle 1431 

control cells and  55 ,735  compound -treated cells across the 62 small -molecule perturbations. 1432

ComBat.  Linear empirical Bayes batch adjustment was performed on the  l𝑜𝑔 1𝑃  expression 1449

3b, c ). The resulting batch -adjusted expression matrix was centered, scaled to unit variance, 1452 

reduced to its top 50 principal components (n_comps=50), and embedded into a 2D UMAP 1453 

representation via a 15 -nearest -neighbor graph. 1454
